# Validation record

## Definition family discovery — 221 (2026-10-28 UTC)

Definitions now separates Records, Measurements and App contracts through the
existing application tab adapter. Tabs preserve mounted editors, filters, exact
revision selections and annotation drafts. Record draft/preview handoffs select
Records. Leaving App contracts cancels its pending HTTP preview; a late response
cannot publish into the hidden pane. Browsing families adds no telemetry reads.

Validation: all 583 frontend tests pass, including real loopback transport tests.
Real Chromium 221 draft/filter/keyboard/workspace-return checks pass at 320/390/700
widths; its delayed real HTTP response cancels by matching query ID and explicit
retry succeeds. Existing 217 source phases/scope/visibility and 110 exact revision,
annotation draft, stale refresh and failed refresh checks pass; 219 catalog and
40 question handoff checks pass. Both updated Bombadil family and legacy-owner
campaigns pass. Current 110/40 frames and the 221 mobile family frame are generated
from the lab. Shared mobile chrome remains the next separate slice.

## Data-first Saved capture catalog — 220 (2026-10-28 UTC)

Saved now places its capture catalog before secondary disclosures, with one
heading and compact refresh/status. Native `goo-table` rows show title, kind and
readable captured UTC; exact clock strings remain in focusable metadata. Manual
ID tools stay below the list. Direct Saved entry loads its first stored page.
Stored order/cursors, publication/retry and selected evidence/drafts are unchanged;
there is no recent-first sorting, data deletion or universal store.

Validation: all 583 frontend tests pass. Real Chromium 220 checks pass for direct
entry, server-page ID/order equality, exact clock metadata, keyboard opening,
source-free inspection, immutable receipt identity, next/previous pages and
320/390/700/701px page bounds. Existing 117 draft/pending-recovery/error journeys
pass. The new bounded Saved Bombadil actor passes typed-row/clock, no-source,
overflow and uncaught-error invariants through stored opening and recovery/list
navigation. Current 220 and affected 117 documentation frames are regenerated.
The first stored row is above 400px at the checked desktop viewport.

The post-R1–R3 review keeps Definitions family discovery and shared mobile chrome
as the next cohesion work. Architecture/store semantics, API schemas and Go code
are unchanged; no new Go/Hegel/Quint campaign was needed for this composition cut.
Only disposable owned review/test outputs are removed; all saved lab volumes remain.

## Workspace-owned declaration startup — 219 (2026-10-28 UTC)

Optional Examples galleries/workflows now mount on first visit and share one
example-question response through independent consumer snapshots. Definitions
loads its legacy package/prebuilt metadata on first use; Operations and Definitions
activate their shared operation declaration load once. Explicit catalog refreshes
stay fresh. No generic cache, dynamic import/bundler, source-query scope change or
new saved object is introduced.

Validation: all 583 frontend tests pass. Five real Chromium 219 checks pass,
covering initial deferral, first-open/revisit retention, direct entry to all three
owners, actual guided telemetry preview, fresh native refresh and the updated
workspace Bombadil duplicate-load/navigation invariants. The isolated generic
startup journey 191 passes with ordinary Python-tagged OTel and no lab assets;
its current frames include the shared compact record styling. One matched local
recording with 90 live rows measured 14 → 7 startup API requests and 3,252 → 2,936
DOM nodes; module count remains 136. These are request/DOM observations, not a
latency or throughput claim. The automatic telemetry stream is unchanged.

Architecture, storyboard, current roadmap and known limits are updated. Two
retention-status startup reads and the eager module graph remain explicit
follow-ons. No Go/Hegel/Quint rerun was needed for this catalog-only JS change.
Temporary generic binaries/test artifacts are removed; saved lab volumes remain.

## Compact shared observation rows — 218 (2026-10-28 UTC)

Compact record summaries now align severity/status, body and duration on one
short desktop scan line. Long bodies preview two complete lines with an ellipsis;
labels do not squeeze into vertical letters. Full noncompact detail remains
available. Shared record CSS owns the behavior and desktop action spacing;
duplicate Activity message-height overrides are removed from product CSS.
No data API, source read, filter, saved format or widget state is added.

Validation: real ordinary-OTLP log/span journey 218 and expanded standalone/nested
SSR/detached-SPA component journey 161 pass in Chromium. Short rows stay under
48px at the checked 1440px viewport; long labels/text, exact UTC tooltips, local
full-text inspection and keyboard focus return pass. Narrow 320/390/700/701px
layouts avoid page overflow and preserve 28px mobile action targets. Journey 102
passes with its observed Quint ledger/negative checks and updated Bombadil
pause/inspect/close and short-row-density invariants. Current 218 desktop/mobile
frames and the affected 102 entry frames are regenerated from the real app.

Updated component docs, storyboard, live-activity guidance and roadmap status.
No new Go/Hegel or whole-Node rerun was needed for this CSS-only production cut;
R1's full frontend suite remains the preceding JS baseline. Firefox/Safari
conformance is not established. Disposable local review/build/campaign artifacts
are removed, while useful documentation frames and all saved lab volumes remain.

## Legacy browser source ownership — 217 (2026-10-28 UTC)

The four admitted legacy source GETs now use decoded `getJSON` transport with
query IDs and cancellation owned through JSON consumption. Python forwards those
IDs for the exact source GET paths, keeping declaration/catalog reads separate.
Operations owns its sequential operation/rate/histogram pipeline; Definitions owns
its HTTP preview. Leave, package/lookback change, document hiding and pagehide stop
pending reads. Stale responses cannot render or launch follow-ons; returning does
not automatically read. Saved publication and SSE lifetimes remain independent.

Validation: all 583 frontend tests and 20 actual-HTTP proxy contracts pass,
including GET cancellation while the upstream JSON body is stalled. The current
Go executable was rebuilt and installed in the local packages lab; the updated
Python/static source was synced to query/packages, restarting only those two
services. Three real Chromium 217 journeys pass: delayed responses at all four
source phases, scope/synthetic-visibility cancellation and explicit retry, plus
the bounded Bombadil actor. Its invariants require an exercised source read,
matching visible owner/query IDs, stopped hidden readers and no uncaught errors.
Campaign directories are isolated per run and successful temporary outputs clean
up automatically; a rerun collision in the first fixed-path harness was corrected.

The storyboard, boundary/architecture flow, API/proxy limits, Bombadil guide and
roadmap status are updated. Existing result encodings and layout are unchanged;
no new screenshot or Quint/Hegel campaign is claimed for this ownership cut. Go
source was unchanged here. Python still buffers JSON and does not forward upstream
Retry-After; cancellation controls are best effort. Temporary binaries/logs and
owned failed-run outputs are removed; saved lab volumes remain intact.

## Whole-product review and roadmap revision (2026-10-28 UTC)

Reviewed source at 9154922, prior specialist/product reviews and real Chromium
Activity, Services/resources, Saved and Definitions surfaces. A delayed real
operation response released after leaving its workspace reproduced hidden
counter-rate and histogram source reads. A single fresh local entry recorded
14 API requests (including duplicate declarations/status), 136 modules and 3,252
DOM nodes; this is a local observation, not a load/performance benchmark. Activity
controls are compact, but short record rows remain vertically expensive.

Current Grafana Explore, Datadog Log Explorer and ClickStack source documentation
were read from public GitHub pages; Honeycomb research remains explicitly limited
to the earlier survey. The combined review and new 2026-10-28 roadmap prioritize
browser ownership, shared observation density and measured startup work before
further specialized capabilities. Old roadmap/backlog headers now point to the
current acceptance index. No product code, saved data or access policy changed in
this review. Temporary review frames are development aids, not a new evidence
bundle. Implementation continues under the user's standing authorization.

## Storage-free supplied measurement evaluation (2026-10-28 UTC)

`measurements.Evaluate(question, rows, at)` now evaluates numeric/state observations
without storage/correction arguments and rejects lifecycle definitions.
`ObserveLifecycle(question, rows, at, custodyDir, previous)` explicitly names the
first-observation publication/correction operation. Shared result preparation
preserves canonical rows, question copies, fixed-time identity and result encoding.
`Source.Run` remains the HTTP adapter's declared mixed read: lifecycle questions
use the configured custody directory; numeric/state questions use pure Evaluate.
The old five-argument Evaluate signature is removed, with all supplied callers
migrated directly and numeric stored replay using the pure API.

Validation: full root Go tests, measurement/native HTTP race suites and focused
vet pass. Hegel measurement state/swarm, container readiness and host authority
properties pass against the migrated API under race detection. The new boundary
regression verifies deterministic fixed-input/time evaluation, stored replay,
lifecycle rejection, wrong-kind observation rejection and no working-directory
file publication. Existing lifecycle first-observation/correction/concurrent
publication and native HTTP/storage contracts pass. OpenAPI regeneration is
byte-identical. No new browser, Bombadil or Quint campaign was needed for this
API-only extraction; UI/SQL/result formats and immutable saved files are unchanged.

Architecture flow, measurement API docs, README, known limits and refactor status
are updated. Lifecycle observation remains a potentially partial publication batch;
existing retry/correction rules apply. Result metadata/hash simplification and
broader source/publication relayering remain separate work. Temporary migration
scripts/test logs are removed; saved lab volumes remain intact.

## Owned instrumentation runtime (2026-10-28 UTC)

`instrumentation.NewRuntime(Config)` now requires explicit meter/tracer providers,
creates all instruments eagerly and returns wrapped construction errors. Direct
Runtime methods own instruments and a bounded outcome-initialization table.
`BindDefault` connects existing woven hooks once per process; nil/zero runtimes
and rebinding are rejected. Pre-binding hooks are no-ops, without evaluating event
selectors. SDK providers/exporter shutdown remain application-owned. Both sample
apps and the Hegel/SDK/woven fixtures use explicit runtime ownership.

Validation: full root Go tests pass. Instrumentation race tests and vet pass,
covering all four constructor failures, nil providers, concurrent exactly-once
completion, isolated direct runtimes, binding races and initialization overflow.
Parallel real-SDK runtimes retain separate spans/events/counts and outcome zeros.
The Hegel state/swarm accounting check passes with fresh providers per case and
invariants for active, completed-call and duration totals. Subject-scope SDK checks
pass. Both sample modules pass race tests and vet; manifests validate for both
samples and the woven example. Actual Orchestrion scenario tests pass, including
success/error/panic telemetry, selector drops and Quint faithful/negative checks.
Both deployable sample binaries also build through their lint-before-weave scripts.

Instrument names/units, scope/version/schema, annotation syntax and stored result
formats are unchanged. Zero baselines are seeded for up to 1,024 distinct
name/role pairs per runtime; later pairs keep actual observations but no synthetic
zeros. This bounds bookkeeping, not SDK series cardinality. No provider copying,
implicit global initialization, rebinding or automatic shutdown is added. Updated
architecture/startup diagrams, developer storyboard and refactor status explain
the prerelease startup change. Temporary build binaries/manifests/logs are removed;
saved lab volumes remain intact. No UI-layout change or browser rerun is claimed.

## Legacy operation source policies (2026-10-28 UTC)

The four legacy source GETs and their implicit HEAD handlers now use family-owned
`internal/operationhttp` declarations for shared source admission/cancellation and
55-second source deadlines after existing validation. The enclosing budget is
60 seconds; the configured client's 15-second per-request bound is unchanged.
`/operation-packages` remains a pure declaration outside admission. Successful
payloads and unsaturated validation/source-error behavior remain unchanged.
OpenAPI now declares optional query IDs and 400/409/429 responses on these GETs.
Admission precedes route validation and capability checks, as with modern reads.

Validation: full root Go tests, command/query race suites, focused vet and OpenAPI
route/schema/fixture checks pass. New tests cover all four GET/HEAD admission
policies, declaration bypass, unrelated methods/paths, actual blocked-source
cancellation, 55-second context propagation, query-ID tombstones, released capacity
and capacity sharing with modern exploration. Policies are checked against the
published GET API and implicit HEAD equivalence. Existing legacy wire-validation
fixtures remain unchanged. API checks report 83 Go operations, 429 fixture
responses, 159 admitted requests and 28 mux refusals, with negative/SSE checks.

Docs and the boundary flow diagram are updated. No UI/semantic computation/storage
format change; no browser, Bombadil, Hegel or Quint rerun for this HTTP policy cut.
Disposable test logs are removed; saved lab volumes remain intact. Mixed
publication/retention routes and SSE are not converted to ordinary source reads.

## Bounded Go executable shutdown (2026-10-28 UTC)

Go `serve-http` now handles SIGINT/SIGTERM, closes its listener and drains active
handlers for five seconds. A drain timeout cancels the server request root and
closes remaining connections, returning an error. Normal drained shutdown returns
success. Configuration-only construction still performs no listen or source probe.
The two Go sample apps run deferred SDK Shutdown after serving, with independent
five-second provider budgets (checkout metrics then traces; reports traces).
Deferred cleanup also runs on later initialization/listen errors. Provider failures
are logged. Compose gives the samples and checkout peer 20 seconds before kill.

Validation: full root Go tests pass; focused shutdown tests pass under the race
detector, covering listener failure, completed-request draining and cancellation/
forced close of an outstanding request. Both sample modules pass race tests and
vet, with actual SDK queued-span and final periodic-metric export tests. Root
command vet passes. The built executable exits successfully on real SIGTERM and
SIGINT after opening its listener. Compose configuration validates. No API, UI,
measurement or stored-data format changes; no browser/Bombadil/Hegel/Quint rerun
was needed for this executable lifecycle cut. Architecture and shutdown flow docs
are updated. Saved volumes remain intact; temporary binaries/logs are removed.

Forced close does not establish whether an interrupted publication committed;
existing retry contracts remain essential. Cancellation is cooperative, not a join
of arbitrary handler goroutines. SIGKILL/crashes cannot flush, and no live collector
shutdown-delivery guarantee is claimed. Python lifecycle and broader legacy-route
admission remain separate work.

## Explanation and capture policy extraction (2026-10-28 UTC)

`internal/explanationhttp` owns observed explanations and pure plan/filter routes;
`internal/capturehttp` owns saved captures, promotion and live-comparison drafts.
Their POST policies supply exact JSON limits, trailing errors and source timeouts
to the custom family handlers and admission. Mixed read/promotion and read/freeze
effects are explicit. Only source branches start their 55-second contexts; held
publication keeps existing admission and retry semantics without a reread. The
source-only generic handler rejects mixed publication effects. Command admission
now composes four family lookups, with no duplicated controlled-path list.

Validation: full root Go tests, focused vet and command/family race checks pass.
Existing strict JSON, source/deadline, capture refresh, publication retry and
server-isolation tests pass through the extracted owners. New checks cover held
publication admission, pure plans during saturation, saved GET separation and
rejection of generic read wrappers for mixed lifetimes. All four families' byte/
admission declarations match OpenAPI. Route/schema/fixture validation passes;
regeneration changes only owner metadata for 11 operations, with no wire difference.

The rebuilt local server passes the exact live-comparison freeze/retry and observed
population-explanation promotion journeys, including Quint trace/negative checks
and both Bombadil campaigns. Architecture flow, boundary inventory, known limits
and refactor status are updated. No intended UI layout, source computation or
storage-format change. Disposable run outputs and incidental screenshot changes
are excluded; durable saved app volumes remain intact. Broader legacy-route
admission and bounded executable shutdown/export flushing remain planned.

## Server-owned snapshot banks (2026-10-28 UTC)

`configuredHTTPServer` now creates one result/explanation bank per server and
passes it explicitly to record/explanation producers, capture promotion and
workbench/retention consumers. The package-global bank is removed. Tail proof
sharing is explicit rather than variadic; comparison drafts remain registration-
local. Existing retention recovery and shared durable capture storage are preserved.
Registration tests own their banks instead of resetting production globals.

Validation: the full root Go suite, command race suite and focused vet pass.
An actual configured-server regression checks owning/foreign record-token loads,
explanation promotion/refusal, same-server promotion idempotence, retention's
unavailable-token recovery ordering, replacement-server token loss and reopening
shared durable captures without source rereads. Its source is a real HTTP fixture;
optional XTDB connection-error paths are tested without a live XTDB transaction.
OpenAPI/schema/fixture checks pass and regeneration is byte-identical.

The rebuilt local server passes the full observed population-explanation journey,
including promotion retry without rereading, Quint trace/negative checks and its
Bombadil campaign. Both live-stream entry and background/scroll ownership journeys
pass. The ownership diagram, architecture, known limits and refactor status are
updated. Existing snapshot TTL/capacity bounds now apply to each server's bank;
this is not authentication or partitioning of configured durable stores. No wire,
storage-format or UI-layout change. Disposable run outputs and incidental
historical screenshot updates are excluded; saved app volumes are preserved.

Explanation/capture policy extraction and bounded executable shutdown/export
flushing remain planned.

## Native HTTP policies and shared typed handlers (2026-10-28 UTC)

`internal/nativehttp` owns native metric/host/pod discovery, measurement execution
and pure host/pod planning. Its seven route policies drive decoding, deadlines and
admission. Record/native families share `queryhttp.Policy`, inferred `ReadHandler`
and `PlanHandler` mechanics. Source deadlines still begin after validation; pure
plan deadlines still begin before decoding and check pre/post computation. The
native execution policy explicitly permits existing lifecycle custody; discovery
and plans do not write it. Catalog/package publication, container planners,
explanation/capture routes, SSE and application snapshot ownership remain separate.

Validation: the full root Go suite, focused vet and race checks pass. Existing
native/record JSON/status/deadline fixtures pass. Deterministic `testing/synctest`
checks verify deadline ordering, parent deadline ceilings and child-context cleanup.
Composition tests cover native admission, pure plans during source saturation,
configured custody/no discovery writes and stable first-observation bytes. Shared
contract checks cover both families' body/admission declarations. OpenAPI route,
schema and fixture checks pass; regeneration changes only seven source-owner
metadata entries, with no wire-contract difference.

The rebuilt local server passes pod-memory and compact-host journeys and the full
ordinary-app measurement/capture/reuse journey, including Quint validation and
negative controls. The Hegel measurement state/swarm check passes, as does the 30-second pod-memory
Bombadil campaign with its existing phase and terminal invariants. The histogram
journey now observes a baseline, triggers another ordinary checkout, and verifies
exported cumulative-count progress; its earlier first-sample assumption could
correctly produce only zero intervals. Product histogram semantics are unchanged.

Architecture diagrams, boundary/planning docs and the measurement storyboard are
updated. No intended UI layout, source schema or storage-format change. Disposable
run outputs and incidental historical screenshot updates are excluded; saved app
volumes are preserved. Broader capture/explanation policy adoption, per-server
snapshot ownership and executable shutdown/export flushing remain planned.

## Record HTTP policy and handler extraction (2026-10-28 UTC)

`internal/recordhttp` now owns the seven record-family routes and their effect,

Validation: the full root Go suite and focused vet checks pass. Race-detector

New composition tests verify pure comparison under exhausted source admission,

The OpenAPI checker passes route/schema/fixture coverage; regeneration changes

The rebuilt local server passes the real core query/capture/discovery/cancel

The API build/check tools share source indexing for extracted typed registration.

## Cancellation through JSON consumption (2026-10-28 UTC)

`queries.mjs` now returns decoded `{ok, status, data}` replies through `postJSON`.
All existing query-helper consumers use it; no raw-response `post` export remains.
The abort listener stays active until JSON completes and is removed on every exit.
Late aborted replies are rejected even when a transport ignores its signal. Owners
retain HTTP error messages and scope/generation guards. Pure planning uses the
same decoder without query IDs. Saves, ambiguous-commit recovery and SSE are
unchanged. Cancellation controls are best effort and do not imply rollback.

Validation: 581 Node tests pass, including six transport regressions covering real
streamed HTTP, pre-abort, late replies, malformed bodies, HTTP/transport failures,
listener cleanup and cancellation-request failure. Eighteen actual HTTP Python
proxy tests pass, including cancellation while upstream JSON remains incomplete.
Go query-budget regressions pass, including cancellation after headers during
body generation and admission cleanup. The question-session Hegel state/swarm
check passes. Chromium passes real streamed-body cancellation, the live
query/capture/discovery/cancel journey with observed Quint trace validation, and
the closed-filesystem late-response journey. The bounded 30-second Bombadil host
workflow passes all phases and invariants. The core journey's status locator
now selects the intended chart caption among nested status regions.

The Python proxy and Go body lifetimes were already correct; production changes
are in the browser helper and its consumers. Broader endpoint policy, server
extraction and shutdown/export work remain planned. Architecture flow and test
instructions are updated. No layout change or performance claim; incidental
historical screenshot updates and disposable run outputs are excluded.

## Unified Go baseline (2026-10-28)

All six modules now declare Go 1.27.0. `.go-version` pins the validated 1.27.2
compiler and selects it for CI, matching the three existing Docker builders.
CI now checks instrumentation and both example modules in addition to root.

Checks on Go 1.27.2 linux/amd64: root `go test ./... -count=1` and `go vet ./...`
pass; instrumentation tests pass. Both examples and both verification modules
compile with `go test ./... -run '^$'`. That compile check does not run native
properties, models or Orchestrion-woven scenarios. No UI or runtime deployment
changed, and no screenshots or additional evidence artifacts were generated.

A temporary old-directive modfile confirmed the root main previously compiled
with Go 1.24 compatibility defaults, including `containermaxprocs=0` and
`updatemaxprocs=0`. With the new directive, `DefaultGODEBUG` is empty: Go 1.27's
normal defaults apply, including container-aware CPU selection/updates unless
explicitly overridden. The repository's Compose/Docker configuration has no
GOMAXPROCS/GODEBUG override. Existing running binaries have not been rebuilt;
no performance claim is made. The temporary comparison files were removed.


## Categorical selected-versus-rest populations (2026-10-23 UTC)

The existing facet tray now offers Compare with rest. A bounded generated
categorical intent is complemented inside each selected signal's unchanged
parent/window; the parent predicate is never negated. Missing versus empty and
qualified fields remain distinct. The action stages a child with exact remainder
baseline questions. Existing explicit Run, comparison, save and capture owners
perform source reads; parent return remains source-free. Unselected companion
questions remain intact. Separate reads do not establish an atomic snapshot.

Actual checks:

- All 42 Node test files pass, including source immutability, companion retention,
  pagination reset, parent preservation and complement-specific predicate budgets.
- Hegel generates categorical include/exclude and same-field OR combinations over
  qualified fields, nested and unfiltered parents (60 examples; 4.5s). Both
  questions compile through Go; exhaustive missing/empty/present record grids
  verify no overlap and selected OR rest equals the parent.
- Source journey 123 passes with a 20-second Bombadil campaign (57.5s). It checks
  actual ClickHouse result memberships for a 2+2 partition and an excluded fifth
  record, versus a one-row UI page, mobile controls, empty rest/no normalization,
  paused-parent return, exact saved baseline configuration and comparison capture.
- Final deployed journeys 123 and existing overlapping comparison/restoration 116
  pass together (1.1m), including saved-view/capture reopening without record
  reads, observed Go/Quint drilldown replay and seven rejecting controls. Mutations
  add hidden reads, change rest environment/filters, narrow the window, alter
  return depth or make the rest overlap the selection. Bombadil checks local
  selection/staging/return adds no record reads after the initial explicit Run.
- Readonly deployed API acceptance passes with matching OpenAPI and seven actual
  handler responses. No runtime Go source or backend wire shape changed.
- Mobile screenshot review exposed the taller sticky trail covering the sticky
  selection summary. The mobile population tray now stays inline; browser
  hit-testing checks its summary is unobscured. Full source polish journey passes
  (39.5s); focused deployed journey 124 passes (6.6s) after positioning the
  containing scroller as in the real documentation flow.
- Actual frames 123a/b and affected 116 frames, README, queue, population contract
  and process diagram describe this bounded capability and its limits.

Deployment used the existing local Go query image with one combined UI/docs COPY
layer. An initial six-COPY build completed but exhausted disk under Docker vfs;
that unused image was removed and the running image tag restored before the
single-layer rebuild/deployment. The service/data were not changed by that failed
disk strategy. This is an asset deployment, not a fresh backend rebuild.
Temporary source/build files and obsolete build cache are removed after checks;
application data, volumes, compact evidence and committed frames are preserved.
Disposable workspace Go compiler cache and all unused Docker compiler/module build
cache were ultimately cleared to recover working space; future cold builds may
need downloads/compilation. The final supported builder prune reported 300.5 MB.

Seven older `/tmp/goo-historical-*` browser archives were outside the normal CLI
root. A reviewed one-off application of the same classifier/TTL/pin rules expired
32,628 raw files while SHA-256 checks verified 1,430 compact files unchanged. Per-
archive deletion inventories and the combined `legacy-retention-applied.json`
remain in artifacts. Allocated-block estimates did not translate to equivalent
free filesystem space; unlinking old layered files did not resolve the shortage.
The normal CLI root restriction remains intact. No application/system tables or
service logs were deleted. Disk remains constrained after supported cache pruning.
An attempted metadata-only Docker image registration was rejected by automatic
approval review as risky; it was not executed or retried. Supported pruning was
used instead.


## Explicit enclosing population time windows (2026-10-23 UTC)

Applied/configured population selectors now disclose an exact UTC expansion
form. It requires a strict enclosing aligned window within the existing
120-minute/pre-2100 limits, preserves complete independent questions and resets
paging. Opening stages through the existing child owner; Run reads explicitly.
The parent retains its paused results/baselines and returns without a reread.
Staged facet drafts must be applied or cleared first. No predicate removal,
selection-versus-rest algebra, new evaluator or API operation is introduced.

Actual checks:

- All 42 Node test files pass, including immutable complete-question retention,
  offset reset and invalid/narrowed/misaligned/over-budget bounds.
- Hegel generated windows compile through Go while preserving independent
  missing/empty field selectors and complete question copies (40 examples; 3.6s).
- Source journey 122 passes with its 20-second Bombadil campaign (43.6s).
  The final rebuilt/deployed journey also passes with saved-view save/reopen:
  two matching spans become three after expanding backwards, with a one-row
  inspection page; companion predicates stay exact. Editing, opening, parent
  return and saved-view reopening do not read telemetry.
- The observed Go/Quint drilldown trace passes; six negative controls reject
  preview/return reads, companion environment drift, changed filters, narrowed
  bounds and invalid return depth. Bombadil opens/edits/stages/returns locally
  while checking no source reads beyond the initial explicit Run.
- The deployed readonly API check passes: matching OpenAPI plus all seven real
  handler responses. No backend wire change or Databricks instance is required.
- The first mobile check found datetime-input overflow; scoped grid/flex sizing
  fixes it, and the final mobile journey passes. Frame 122 shows the actual
  compact UTC preview. The existing journey 116 needed its parent-summary CSS
  locator scoped after adding a nested disclosure; this is a test composition
  change, not altered comparison execution. Its final deployed rerun passes (21.1s).

README, active plan, population contract/process diagram, journey index and
known limits are synchronized. Cleanup removes only obsolete build layers/images
and eligible test recordings; application data and durable evidence are retained.
The temporary source UI is stopped.


## Development browser diagnostic retention (2026-10-23 UTC)

Added a dry-run-first artifacts-only cleanup command, age-based raw recording
expiry and a 512 MiB soft budget. Successful recordings expire after 24 hours
(or after two quiet hours under budget pressure), explicit failures after seven
days and legacy/unknown recordings after 72 hours. Pins, active markers and
fresh bundle files protect recordings. Compact evidence/logs and unknown formats
remain; symlinks are not followed. Bombadil records outcomes atomically, and the
journey wrapper prunes after restoring traffic without replacing the test status.
Application telemetry, stores, receipts, documents and committed screenshots are
outside the tool's root and are not cleaned.

Actual checks and application:

- Seven filesystem/wrapper tests pass: deadline boundaries, budget precedence,
  pins/active runs/fresh logs, symlinks, changed bundles, hardlink accounting,
  compact/model evidence preservation, and traffic restoration with simultaneous
  test and cleanup failures. Bash/Node syntax and whitespace checks pass.
- Applied the reviewed plan: 69 expired bundles, 23,792 raw files removed, zero
  skipped files; 1,185,822,637 logical bytes and an estimated 202,919,936 allocated
  bytes reclaimed. Hardlinks explain the difference. The compressed deletion
  inventory is under `artifacts/retention/20261023T020321Z-79185304.jsonl.gz`.
- SHA-256 checks verify all 3,517 inventoried compact files remain unchanged.
- Real ordinary-OTel journey 121 and its 20-second Bombadil campaign pass (36.3s).
  Its outcome file reports passed and no active marker remains. A deliberately
  nonexistent Bombadil specification exits unsuccessfully, reports failed and
  clears its active marker as well. This validates both metadata branches.
- A subsequent dry run proposes no further deletion. After the verification
  campaign, retained raw files occupy about 419 MB physically, under 512 MiB.
  No Docker image rebuild or application retention change was needed.

The budget covers raw recordings, not the entire artifacts directory. Recent
failed/unknown runs and pins can exceed it; reports expose that condition. Logs
and compact ledgers remain unbounded, and there is no background scheduler.
Direct Playwright calls need explicit pruning. See
[policy and commands](docs/DIAGNOSTIC_RETENTION.md).

## Descriptive comparison ranking (2026-10-23 UTC)

Shared pure ranking now orders live and stored groups by exact absolute count,
share, recorded error-rate or mean/p95/p99 changes. It preserves full support
counts and unknown/zero-support measures, ranks missing comparisons last and
uses stable value-identity ties. Stored selected-measure bars share one domain;
rounded duration labels retain raw values in tooltips. Live selected ranking and
baseline configuration round-trip through saved exploration views, including the
newly admitted `count` metric. Stored receipt choices remain local presentation.
Questions, comparison arithmetic, receipts and row-page-independent totals remain
unchanged. No significance/support-threshold or causal claim is introduced.

- Hegel ordering/purity and saved-view state/swarm properties pass (4.0s), including
  rank-choice changes across immutable saved revisions. They reuse the existing
  store protocol; no new evidence clock or execution model is introduced.
- Ordinary OTel journey 120 passes with its 20-second Bombadil campaign (39.5s):

- Existing span capture/share and ordinary-log journeys pass together (50.8s),

- Browser review found cramped value/action columns; their minimum widths now

Cleanup before work removed unused build cache (reported 601.5 MB), increasing

  validate against event schemas. Batch examples/negative controls also validate;

  64 operations load, filtering/disclosure and displayed limits work, no mobile
  horizontal page overflow and no page errors. A real tail-contract screenshot

- Go schema generation plus spec assembly is byte-identical on regeneration.

- Whitespace and local Markdown targets pass. Isolated validation dependencies are

Evidence: `artifacts/openapi-{contract,python,wire-race,browser,root}.log`.

## Compact record/native definition rows — first B1 slice (2026-10-22 UTC)

Final image: `sha256:0d60f896bc4f2a15b893b7f8cd56b368c4f71d6bc6a755dece2086ecb7684f08`.
`GooDefinitionLibrary` now composes compact typed exact-revision rows, local loaded-
catalog search/paging and one selected inspector in both Definitions catalogs.
Record annotation panels mount only in selected detail; the inspector's bounded
cache preserves visited local drafts. Selection does not validate/execute/publish or
replace the package editor draft. Same-title definitions remain separate. Explicit
Edit/Explore/Preview actions retain their existing typed owner contracts. Native
refreshes now reject older responses; failed refreshes retain old rows/detail.

This is a bounded first B1 cut, not completion of the whole milestone. The fixed
reusable-question list and compatible document/retained catalog adoption remain
open, as does subsequent touched retention-owner separation. No backend cursor,
family/lineage inference, global search or new recovery promise is introduced.
[Contract and ownership diagram](docs/DEFINITION_LIBRARY.md).

## Live span latency and recorded error share (2026-10-20 UTC)

Saved span questions requesting duration/status now compare count, compact
mean/p50/p95/p99 and recorded Error/total share under one rolling window and
current evidence basis. The existing count aggregate already returns these
measures in one SQL statement. Current refresh responses expose current/baseline
basis metadata without changing historical comparison receipt formats. Freeze
preserves the complete original result without another source read.

## Rolling record baseline and passive chart frame (2026-10-20 UTC)

Saved span/log questions now support explicit rolling full-population count
refresh beside frozen linked evidence. The app owns cancellation, generations,
source-window labels, stale retention and explicit axis rescaling. Freeze
publishes the displayed server snapshot without another telemetry read; linking
it stages an investigation draft and saving creates an immutable child revision.

detached SPA metadata, native child/focus retention and no source lifecycle.

  zero/unknown distinctions; native interval/histogram semantics remain separate.

## Shared facets and typed attributes (2026-10-20 UTC)

Explore live/frozen distributions now compose a caller-owned `goo-facet-list`.
Local search hides rows without changing supplied exact counts, scales, ranking
or action state. No local matches is distinct from no captured source records.
Attribute inspectors now reuse `goo-field-value` and a passive
`goo-attribute-list`, keeping qualified mapping, search, clipboard feedback and
query actions in the existing app adapter. Values preserve explicit presence,
null/empty/false, exact integer strings, safe text, lazy nested batches and a raw
value escape. Standalone SSR and detached SPA use are documented.

  absence/type distinctions, JSON rejection, cyclic/unsupported input and
  accessor/inherited serialization hooks without executing hooks.
- The initial live campaign passed eight browser tests: component conformance,
  log/trace discovery, grouped measures, question workbench and UI inspection.
  Discovery/group/workbench observed Quint ledgers and rejecting controls passed;
  30-second grouped and attribute-inspection Bombadil campaigns passed with new
  facet visibility/presence invariants.
- Standalone checks cover SSR/pre-upgrade properties, detached values,
  reconnects, safe text and native facet row/state retention without API reads.
  Live inspection verifies scoped duplicate keys, literal strings, clipboard,
  no-match status, original-window narrowing and trace/log/mobile adoption.
- Source reads, query ASTs and Go semantics were unchanged. A parallel agent
  extracted and reviewed typed fields; review caught an inherited array
  serialization hook and a page/component CSS precedence issue, both corrected.
- Final post-review campaign passed all seven browser tests, including new
  invalid-property and 50-to-75-item lazy-expansion assertions, corrected
  trace-facet layout, updated grouped/inspection Bombadil campaigns and Quint
  discovery/group trace checks. Real storyboards and synthetic component frames

- Runtime checks passed: all 12 Compose services running; health OK; served

- Field snapshot updates reset disclosures. Facet row patches require explicit
  `refresh()`; there is no mutation observer or live transport. Broader
  screen-reader validation remains outstanding as documented for the library.

## Passive tables and explicit result states (2026-10-20 UTC)

`goo-table` and `goo-result-state` now provide native caller-owned table viewports
and supplied state displays in Explore and Services. Captions, scoped headers,
keyboard overflow and compact density share one implementation. Source reads,
full totals, paging and action callbacks remain in the app. Native row patches,
attribute changes and reconnects retain existing row/action identity; the component
has no subscription or query behavior. Draft/cancel paths clear loading state.
The design subagent implemented Services adoption and reviewed the shared contract.

## Framework-neutral display components and compact exploration (2026-10-20 UTC)

The public library now registers `goo-latency`, `goo-stat-list` and
`goo-metric-comparison`, using ordinary server HTML attributes and detached SPA
properties. Its standalone `/ui-components` reference has synthetic fixtures and
loads no app runtime/query client. Services and live/frozen Explore reuse the
latency/stat primitives; stored comparisons group mean/P95/P99 on one matrix.
Only supplied percentiles are shown; no P90 evaluator was introduced. Query
construction now occupies one compact workbench, and applied measures do not
force advanced settings open. Widget secondary actions use native disclosure.

The [whole-app design audit](docs/UI_COMPONENT_AUDIT.md) prioritizes passive tables,
result states, facets and typed attributes next. It also specifies eventual SSE
and baseline-vs-live boundaries. These additional elements and streaming features
remain proposed. Current summary updates replace inner DOM/reset disclosures;
they are not high-rate focus/scroll-preserving tailers.

explicit recaptures of the original fixed windows. Frozen receipts use algorithm
v3; v1/v2 receipts reopen without reevaluation. Results precede provenance,
with readable duration/error displays and internally scrolling mobile tables.

## Observed native window discovery (2026-10-19)

[Find comparable windows](docs/OBSERVED_WINDOW_DISCOVERY.md) reads validated
retained kernel intervals without a telemetry query. It intersects boundaries
across all retained streams, previews exact support and exclusions, requires
explicit selection on both sides and rereads two new exact questions. New results
are compared through the existing engine; original captures remain unchanged.

`window-discovery-vet-final.log`); all **42 frontend tests**

state/swarm scenarios of 15 steps** (`window-discovery-hegel-final.log`);
verification vet and Quint model typecheck. Unit coverage includes source-offline
HTTP discovery, strict request validation, reset taint, all-member coverage,
asynchronous boundaries, layout changes, empty histograms, aggregate overflow,
10,000 intervals and explicit 32-window/64-exclusion output bounds.

The first Hegel invocation omitted the configured writable XDG cache and failed

## Complete native window comparisons (2026-10-19)

[Native window comparison](docs/NATIVE_WINDOW_COMPARISON.md) uses complete validated
coverage for exact OTel streams. Counter rates use exact window increments and
actual durations; histogram comparisons combine compatible bucket populations
and retain p95 ranges. Missing members, resets, gaps, incomplete boundaries,
temporality changes, incompatible layouts and histogram count overflow withhold
changes. One unsupported member withholds all deltas. New receipts use algorithm
v2; v1 snapshots remain readable without reevaluation.

Passed local checks: full root Go suite including counter exact totals beyond
uint64, histogram overflow refusal, whole-population p95s, multi-stream withholding
and v1 receipt compatibility (`artifacts/native-windows-go-final.log`); 40 frontend
checks (`native-windows-node.log`); 100 requested Hegel exact-rate,
share-conservation/layout-refusal, reversal and immutability cases plus 60 saved
comparison state/swarm scenarios of 15 steps (`native-windows-hegel-complete.log`);
verification vet and Quint model typecheck (`native-windows-vet-final.log`,
`native-windows-model.log`). An initial property filename used the Go Windows-only
suffix and was renamed before accepting results. The first actual run then exposed
an incomplete test stream fixture; adding its required instrumentation scope fixed
that fixture. Both named Hegel tests executed and passed afterward.

Focused live journey: **1 passed, no skips, 1.3 minutes**
(`artifacts/native-windows-focused.log`). It ingests real standard OTLP delta and
cumulative sums and explicit histograms, compares complete windows, shares a v2
snapshot in a fresh browser, checks mobile layout and refuses counter decrease,
coverage gaps and bucket mismatch. Live Quint replay passed with six rejecting
mutations; 30 seconds of Bombadil frozen-result exploration passed. Initial ledger:
`artifacts/native-windows/1792448783045/ledger.json`.

Compatibility campaign: **5 passed, no skips, 3.6 minutes**
(`artifacts/native-windows-release.log`), covering record/gauge comparison 52,
native windows 54 and saved sharing/recovery 53. All enabled Bombadil exploration
and live Quint checks passed. Visual review then improved native table scrolling
on mobile and reused the existing captured-attribute viewer for stream identity.
The final affected journey passed again (**1 passed, no skips, 1.1 minutes**,
`native-windows-polish-focused.log`), including attribute disclosure and actual
horizontal scrolling, plus six rejecting trace controls and Bombadil. Its ledger
is `artifacts/native-windows/1792449184577/ledger.json`. Five new focused frames and

Final health confirms 12 running services, functional query health, restored owned

## Metric-only service discovery (2026-10-19)

without combining metric values, units or histogram observations. A direct Services
entry opens the catalog; metric rows open the original fixed window in service

Added explicit int64/uint64 attribute/resource bindings, canonical-decimal parsing,
declared units, exact reference sum/extrema/rational-mean states and separate
valid/missing/empty/invalid counts. A source-free HTTP preflight reports structured
capability diagnostics and always marks execution unavailable. Live numeric SQL,
UI controls and package/capture persistence remain future work; existing executable
question/package/capture formats are unchanged. See [contract and process diagram](docs/NUMERIC_ATTRIBUTE_PLANNING.md).

Passed: full root Go suite, exploration/HTTP race tests, root vet, Python compilation
and Hegel partition conservation (100 requested cases). Tests include signed and
unsigned limits, sums above uint64, zero versus unavailable extrema, malformed
syntax, bad unit labels, detached scope and strict HTTP decoding. Reproducible live
proxy checks passed with `python3 scripts/check_numeric_preflight.py`, including
supported declarations, multiple unsupported diagnostics and unknown-field rejection.
No UI behavior changed, so browser journeys/screenshots and Bombadil/Quint live
ledgers were not regenerated for this source-free milestone.

## 2026-10-21 — explicit histogram time buckets and whole-product component sweep

Implemented opt-in `histogramBucketMs` in native histogram packages and
`metrics.BucketHistograms` over evaluated exports. Half-open buckets anchor at
exact question start, retain a possibly shorter final window, and combine only
whole compatible intervals with complete coverage. Checked native bucket sums
produce exact populations/P95 ranges. Crossings, gaps, unknown/reset diagnostics,
layout changes, invalid counts and overflow withhold the entire affected bucket;
valid neighbors remain usable. Width belongs to the question/revision/basis.
Existing definitions/results omit the new fields and retain their transport shape.

The UI locally switches fixed-time/export views using the existing passive chart,
timeline, grid and inspector. Source reads and capture replay remain in adapters
and Go. Export diagnostics are labelled separately from bucket coverage. Selected
zero/unavailable buckets have explicit guidance and no invented percentile or
stale distribution. Added the executable journey81 storyboard and desktop/mobile
frames, architecture diagram, component/verification notes and documented budgets.

## 2026-10-21 — shared evidence-summary primitive

Implemented passive `goo-evidence-summary` with bounded supplied facts, separate
mode/result state, a visible completeness/independent-read caveat and native
exact-detail disclosure. The application presenter `GooEvidenceScope` interprets
typed measurement receipts; the component owns no reads, actions, subscriptions,
clock inference, navigation or persistence. Adopted in the full measurement capture,
linked native question evidence and a separate native capture card in notes.
Repeated capture scope/count/read paragraphs are suppressed where the summary
owns them; operational refresh/error/attachment status remains adapter-owned.

The presenter preserves all four namespace selector states, exact source counts,
question bounds, declared grain, binding and revision identity. Source read and
stored custody clocks are separately labelled. Missing receipt-only basis/read
metadata remains unavailable; no full-result fetch or custody substitution fills
it in. Full lifecycle results may additionally supply business evaluation and
knowledge-cutoff clocks. Invalid snapshots fail closed and clear prior facts/mode.

Completed validation:

- All 25 frontend test files passed. New pure cases cover bounds/invalid data,
  detached facts, mode/state independence, counts above JS safe integer range,
  exact nanosecond bounds, namespace distinctions and missing source-read clocks.
  Artifact: `artifacts/evidence-summary-frontend.log`.
- Final browser campaign: five tests passed in 2.1m: standalone journey83, real
  OTLP summary journey82, both linked-evidence journey51 cases (record and native),
  and shared Measurements journey38. The first campaign additionally passed
  histogram time-bucket journey81 with unchanged native semantics. Artifact:
  `artifacts/evidence-summary-final-journeys.log`.
- Standalone browser contracts cover pre-definition properties, SSR attributes,
  detached getter isolation, safe text, invalid state clearing, native keyboard
  disclosure, focus/open-state preservation during valid updates, no component
  buttons/links/inputs and mobile overflow.
- Real OTLP capture → linked investigation checks retain exact source/storage
  clocks, source counts, matching-scope guidance and receipt-only limitations.
  Local inspection preserves unsaved findings, performs no source POSTs and
  leaves the saved capture unchanged. Desktop/mobile frames 82a/82b/82c and the
  clearly synthetic frame 83 were generated from the browser and reviewed.
- New observed Quint replay passed in 13.56s with eight rejecting corruptions:
  evidence mode, clock roles/values, local reads, result state/count, linked read
  metadata and immutable reopening. Actual ledger/log:
  `artifacts/evidence-summary/637efe06fe`.
- The saved-capture/investigation Bombadil actor completed 581 trace entries,
  exercised all four required custom actions and passed passive-content,
  supplied frozen mode/state, overflow, exception and completion properties.
- Existing observed Measurements and linked-evidence Quint checks also passed;
  actual ledgers/logs: `artifacts/measurements/b0c8860d` and
  `artifacts/linked-evidence/1792543434585`.
- The first campaign caught a test-driver retry that overwrote navigation IDs
  after the initial hash changed. It was fixed by retaining IDs and making repeat
  navigation idempotent; completion bounds were unchanged. The old native linked
  test expected one source-read paragraph; it now checks the rendered definition

- JavaScript syntax checks and `git diff --check` passed. No production Go evaluator
  or storage schema changed; backend semantic/race and Hegel suites were not

  continue to validate/replay typed Go results through the observed trace tests.

Updated README, architecture diagram, component/adoption contracts, whole-product

## 2026-10-21 — compact service evidence and readable UTC timestamps

Implemented shared summaries for supported record/native service widgets through
`GooEvidenceScope.serviceWidget`. Widgets lead with observations/charts/actions;
one collapsed scope/evidence disclosure holds their summary, read basis and typed
question/source warnings. Reduced summary/card/latency spacing and stopped short
cards stretching to match neighbors. The overview keeps independent-read,
unverified-coverage and capture-reread guidance visible. No planner, query budget,
source mapping or capture persistence schema changed.

Fixed widgets use fixed mode, with record `capturedAt` and native `readAt` treated
as source-read clocks, never custody. Exact counts and namespace distinctions
survive. Shared summaries show UTC timestamps/ranges and shorter same-day bounds;
raw nanoseconds and RFC3339 fractions remain in focusable tooltips and display
snapshots. Native stream tables, endpoint/interval disclosures and selected
interval details use the same timestamp formatter. Durations/counts retain their
native units; no query bounds are rounded.

and the existing passive summary inside the scope/receipt disclosure. Exact
counts, windows, predicate/binding mode and separate read/custody clocks remain
inspectable. Missing source reads never borrow storage time; unavailable basis
metadata is not fabricated or fetched. Gauge endpoint/native-window times and
fresh native bounds show UTC with exact nanosecond tooltips. Fresh baseline
selection also no longer substitutes storage time for a missing source read.

## 2026-10-23 — bounded ClickHouse console logs and disk recovery

A read-only Docker storage inventory identified a 13.4 GB ClickHouse JSON console
log. Preserved its last 200 lines in `/tmp/goo-clickhouse-console-tail.log`, then
removed and recreated only the ClickHouse container using Compose, retaining the
existing named database volume. `compose.yaml` now sets `json-file` rotation to
`max-size: 10m`, `max-file: 3`. No application tables or volumes were deleted.

`docker compose config --quiet` passed. The recreated container became healthy;
its inspected logging configuration matched the limits, and a live system-table
query found the existing nine default-database tables. The UI returned HTTP 200.
Actual main-filesystem availability rose from approximately zero to 13 GB after
restart. Development venv and onboarding image staging were also relocated to
`/tmp` with original-path symlinks; file-size inventories matched and imports of
chdb, pandas and pyarrow passed. Those relocations alone recovered little main-disk
space; removal of the oversized console log provided the substantial recovery.

## 2026-10-23 — S3/F1 Activity membership prerequisite

[The prerequisite contract](docs/TAIL_SELECTION_CUSTODY.md) records the existing
capture/result-page boundary, a new bounded Go membership primitive, the proposed
tail publication/retry contract and a clearly planned user storyboard/diagram.
The primitive is unwired: no SSE field, endpoint, UI retention control or retry
journal was enabled. Existing v1 retained manifests still reject a tail origin.

`TailMembershipSnapshots` freezes server read provenance and canonical occurrence
payloads, with an opaque capability and expiry anchored to the original read.
Resolution is all-or-nothing across 1..20 references from one connection/selector;
it preserves each member's window, read clock, basis and late/limited diagnostics.
Input/output mutation, changed selectors/connections, duplicate occurrence aliases,
missing members, expired proof, delayed reissuance and restart cannot substitute
new evidence. Count/byte capacity refuses new issuance without evicting valid proof.
This is ephemeral observation membership, not durable custody or physical event ID.

Actual checks:
- Go exploration, retention and Go HTTP suites passed. Initial checks caught a
  helper naming error and a reserved Quint action name; both were corrected.
  A sandboxed broad tail check could not bind its HTTP test listener; the
  authorized test-server run passed. Final focused membership checks passed.
- Membership unit tests cover exact large numeric tokens, duplicate payload
  occurrences, per-poll clocks, late/limited flags, immutable copies, malformed/
  oversized batches, count/byte bounds, scope, selection bounds, expiry and restart.
- Go race detection passed concurrent issuance/resolution and all membership cases.
- Hegel membership state/swarm passed 70 generated sequences with 30 steps and TTL
  invariants after every action; existing tail overlap/multiplicity properties passed.
- Observed Quint issue/resolve/expire/refuse replay passed, with four controls
  rejecting forged counts, expired availability, member windows and accepting
  unavailable proof. This model does not claim an unimplemented durable publisher.
- Three real local XTDB integration checks passed: interrupted publication/retry,
  concurrent request recovery, and recovery of an already committed selection after
  fixed-page expiry. They verify existing saved/result-page origins only. Other
  optional ClickHouse/XTDB integration opt-ins in the broad suite were skipped,
  not counted as passes.
- The existing real-OTel live-tail journey 66 passed in 1.3 minutes, including its
  observed Quint replay and 30-second Bombadil campaign. The production tail UI/
  transport remains unchanged; frames 66a/b/c were refreshed from the running lab.
- Legacy HTTP rejects tail-reference fields and client rows. The v1 manifest guard
  rejects a self-consistent hash with a fabricated tail origin.
- OpenAPI conformance passed: 58 Go operations, 222 response fixtures, 56 admitted
  requests, 14 mux refusals, negative/SSE controls. No public wire schema changed.

Compact logs are in `artifacts/tail-membership-contract`; current live-tail
ledgers/Bombadil evidence remain with journey 66. New public functionality is not
advertised from this prerequisite. No runtime rebuild is needed for the unwired
bank; current docs/storyboards are synced separately. Owned scratch logs are
archived and cleaned; no telemetry or retained evidence is removed.

Next: implement a distinct typed tail manifest/idempotent durable publisher and
origin-aware resolution, preserving old receipt bytes. Only after committed retry,
changed-intent, expiry, outage and concurrency checks pass should SSE-issued
references and the browser selection/retry workflow be connected.

## 2026-10-23 — origin-aware tail catalog and shared review readers

Implemented Go `ReadEvidence` / `CatalogEvidence` with explicit saved-capture,
result-page and tail-selection origins. Tail evidence retains its typed manifest;
no synthetic captured page/question is created. Catalog items explicitly name
capture or recorded-custody clocks. Shared history, exact review resolution and
idempotent corrections now support initial tail reviews retained in request
receipts, including older rows without lookup projections. Public manifest/catalog
routes and browser inspectors remain v1; no live selection/publication route is
added. See the updated diagram and boundaries in `docs/TAIL_SELECTION_CUSTODY.md`.

Actual checks:

- `GOO_XTDB_TEST_URL=… go test -race ./retention -count=1 -v` passed against local
  XTDB on 15432, including all existing retention integration tests and the new
  shared-reader integration. The new fixture removes its own request projection
  to emulate the previous publisher, resolves its root review, publishes/retries
  a correction, pins the old revision, validates an exact document citation and
  loads the file document offline. It verifies immutable original receipt/manifest
  bytes, expired-bank independence, unknown/cross-target review and parent refusal,
  unavailable-store refusal and legacy catalog isolation.
- Pure catalog checks cover explicit clock boundaries, exact origin/service/tag
  filters, missing/empty service identity, 20-item pagination, stale/cross-filter
  cursors, duplicate identities, missing custody and branch withdrawal behavior.
  Shared pagination/service extraction replaces duplication. A byte fixture was
  frozen by running the original catalog function from commit `b8f6ddc` before
  extraction; the new implementation reproduces that legacy cursor exactly.
  The temporary reference implementation was removed after capturing the fixture.
- `go test ./exploration ./cmd/goo -count=1` passed, including existing HTTP wire
  fixtures. Optional ClickHouse source integration was not enabled; these results
  do not claim ClickHouse or deployed-browser acceptance.
- In `verification`, with the real XTDB opt-in,
  `go test -run 'Test(TailManifestProperties|QuintObservedTailReviews|QuintObservedTailPublication)$' -v -count=1`
  passed. Hegel runs 50 manifest properties, now including typed-origin refusal.
  Actual observed Quint review replay executes publish → correct → withdraw →
  retry-original and checks current heads/active state/catalog visibility,
  immutable pinned review and original observation bytes. Four altered traces
  are rejected. The separate durable-publication replay and its four controls
  also passed. The existing membership state/swarm suite is unchanged.
- OpenAPI conformance passed: 58 Go operations, 222 response fixtures, 56 admitted
  requests, 14 mux refusals, negative controls and SSE schemas. No public response
  union or new route was added; meaning-revision wire shapes are unchanged.
- `git diff --check` passed. Documentation, plan, API boundary notes and the custody
  diagram distinguish shared backend support from the pending browser adapters.
  No UI changed; no browser/Bombadil run, screenshots or runtime image rebuild is
  claimed. Existing lab containers were left running unchanged.

Compact logs are in `artifacts/tail-shared-readers/{race,go,models,openapi}.txt`.
No temporary source/reference implementation, staging directory or owned background
process remains. No telemetry, retained evidence or Docker volume was deleted.

Next: explicit public manifest/catalog and citation presentation adapters, followed
by SSE-issued capabilities and the Activity selection/retry workflow. Initial tail
roots use bounded receipt scans (1,000 requests / 16 MiB); exceeding that budget
refuses instead of returning a partial review history/catalog. Recorded custody
metadata is not an ingestion clock or a claim of an atomic cross-query snapshot.

routes, receipt bytes and catalog cursors remain supported. No tail publication

A shared display-only adapter composes existing record summaries, UTC descriptions,
evidence summaries and attribute viewers; attributes/raw JSON stay behind local
disclosures. Numeric tokens beyond JavaScript round-trip precision are explicit

question is synthesized for tail observations.

## Activity membership, exact selection and retry integration — 2026-10-23

Implemented one startup-injected membership bank shared by trusted SSE issuance
and `POST /retained-evidence/tail`. Resets identify the original connection;
nonempty batches either issue frozen capability metadata or explicitly report
unavailable preservation while observations continue. Browser snapshots retain
per-row connection, read window/read clock, limited/late state and expiry.
Selecting pauses the stream and reveals compact controls for up to 20 occurrences;
Resume replacement clears selection without rebinding references. Publication
accepts only references and journals immutable intent before sending. Submitted
retry remains available after expiry/reload; payload edits are locked after send.

Executed checks:

- `go test -race ./cmd/goo ./exploration ./retention` passed with real local XTDB.
  New native HTTP tests prove actual source-poll issuance, exact numeric tokens,
  connection mismatch refusal, strict input guards, committed recovery with no
  bank after restart, changed-intent conflict and absent-proof 410. A final focused
  race run also proves exhausted issuance capacity keeps streaming observations
  without advertising a token.
- Hegel membership state/swarm and durable manifest properties passed, alongside
  observed Quint membership and actual XTDB publication replay with rejecting
  controls. Two focused Node files passed for bounded selection/provenance,
  expiry/connection/scope refusal, buffer independence and immutable journal reload.
- Real browser journeys 66 and 139 passed (1.4 minutes). Journey 66 preserves late
  duplicates, FIFO limits, focused inspection, pause/resume and reconnect semantics.
  Journey 139 uses two actual OTLP log occurrences in ClickHouse through the
  primary compact Activity entry, selects both, resumes/clears, selects again,
  checks mobile overflow and commits through the real HTTP/XTDB publisher. It
  drops the response *after* commit, reloads Saved, retries via the visible pending
  notice and observes unchanged manifest, review and custody timestamp, with zero
  telemetry rereads after selection. An initial test-only attempt waited on a
  hidden Saved-tools summary; the final test uses the actual visible recovery
  notice. No positive UI data or successful publisher response is mocked.
- The resulting browser ledger passed observed Quint replay; five controls reject
  extra source reads, duplicate publication count, changed custody time, changed
  connection and altered member provenance. Bombadil's 25-second real browser run
  selects, clears and resumes without publishing or generating workload; bounded
  selection, pause state, overflow and exception invariants passed. Screenshots
  139a–c and affected live-tail frames were regenerated from the running app.
- OpenAPI conformance passed: 61 Go operations / 67 total, 222 frozen responses,
  56 admitted requests, 14 mux refusals, updated SSE capability coherence controls
  and 26 actual observed responses including original/recovered tail publication.
  Native semantic validation remains authoritative beyond reflected wire shapes.

Compact logs and API observations are under `artifacts/tail-selection-integration`;
journey ledgers/model controls/Bombadil are under `artifacts/tail-selection`, with
final primary-entry run `tail-select-73d84f71`. Capability bounds, API, architecture,
component ownership, plan, known issues, README and storyboard are synchronized.
The optional store remains required for publication; process-local proof disappears
on restart. This is observation custody, not physical event identity, full trace

## Bounded Activity retention failure acceptance — 2026-10-23

[Acceptance contract](docs/ACTIVITY_RETENTION_ACCEPTANCE.md) now closes the bounded
S3/F1 interaction. Shared pure `GooRetentionStatus` distinguishes unsent local
failure, unknown commit outcome, unconfigured retention, refusal/conflict and
server-confirmed unavailable uncommitted proof. The client preserves HTTP status
and pre-send refusal metadata without changing journal bodies. Activity explains
configuration/expiry and the existing required tag-or-note contract before sending;
rows remain inspectable when proof is unavailable. Check retention only reads
configuration. Periodic expiry updates preserve unchanged live text and the
committed link DOM; retry/clear preserve keyboard focus. Old selection completions
cannot replace a newer selection's status. Saved recovery shares the status policy
but does not promise held observations after reload.

Executed checks:

- Six real browser scenarios (140–145) passed in 1.4 minutes: browser-clock expiry
  with no publication, actual unconfigured owner/failed check/recheck, real XTDB
  TCP transport failure, local journal quota refusal, genuine 64-batch capacity
  exhaustion and actual Go restart losing uncommitted proof. Retry after browser
  expiry sends unchanged intent and succeeds against restored real storage while
  server proof remains valid. Source reads after paused selection remain zero.
  The final Saved-context wording/mobile keyboard test was repeated and passed.
- The instances run the actual Go CLI and Python proxy over independently checked
  OTLP/ClickHouse records. A TCP forwarder breaks only their XTDB connection; no
  successful SSE/publication payload is mocked. Only a failed configuration GET
  and local storage quota are browser fault injections. Owned children, sockets
  and temporary stores close in `finally`. Initial browser checks exposed and
  corrected the new asset's missing Python allowlist entry and an empty annotation
  submission that was refused before reaching storage.
- Hegel's actual membership state/swarm (70 histories × 30 steps) and observed
  membership Quint replay passed. The observed 142 browser outage/retry ledger
  passes Quint replay; five controls reject false 410, hidden source reads,
  disabled submitted retry, changed refs and publication before acknowledgment.
  Its expiry/pending/retry fields come from browser observations. Bombadil's
  25-second capacity-refused inspection campaign passed its no-selection,
  no-overflow, inspection-progress and exception invariants.
- Four focused Node test files passed; Python docs handler's two checks and syntax
  checks passed. Existing OpenAPI conformance remains 61 Go / 67 total operations,
  222 frozen responses, 56 admitted requests, 14 mux refusals and SSE controls.
  The final deployed API docs browser check passed all 67 operations, desktop
  capture, mobile layout and no page errors. Five deployed UI assets match the
  checkout byte-for-byte.

Screenshots 140–145 and the acceptance diagram, README, current plan, known issues,
component ownership and storyboards are synchronized. Compact checks live in
`artifacts/activity-retention-acceptance`; observed per-scenario ledgers, verification
logs and Bombadil output live under `artifacts/tail-acceptance`.

The final query/packages image is
`sha256:a430e567509531187c791750802292295b25abcc8ec3a5f8bf8d0f1c0a1ea570`.
Only query/packages are recreated. Intermediate image/tag and unused regular build
cache are removed through Docker CLI; telemetry, evidence and retention volumes
are preserved. Owned staging is removed after final checks. Browser time advance

Conflict/refusal messages have unit coverage, without claiming a browser
concurrency-conflict or distributed-bank campaign.

Final deployed compatibility check: journeys 66, 99 and 139 all passed, covering

### Trace search — nullable witness tie correction

Source review after the initial source commits found a real tie mismatch: a
derived exception_type classified absent because several types were reported
still carried an internal first-type string. That hidden string could rank ahead
of a later visible resource field when otherwise-identical witnesses tied. A
real ClickHouse counterexample selected visible zone z instead of reference a.
Reviewed correction `618767f` normalizes every absent projected payload to the
empty string in both witness output and argMin ordering, while public fieldValues
still report null. The parser refuses nonempty hidden payloads for absent fields.
No DTO or existing record/capture format changes.

The targeted Go/actual ClickHouse checks pass (0.145s: original matrix 0.10s, new
absent-derived tie 0.04s), including canonical-positive and corrupted-hidden-value
controls. Final related Go regressions pass (exploration 0.834s, cmd/goo 0.493s,
workbench cached). The pre-fix failure and post-fix success are distinct executed
results. Browser acceptance was held until the reviewed fix and replacement
binary were ready; earlier deployed asset checks are not final binary evidence.

### Trace search — independent native and observed-model gates

Both actual baselines pass on their first native execution, with no observer
or fixture correction: main 8.396s (62 rejecting controls) and owner 7.905s
(18). All 80 controls prove a serialized change before rejection. Four bad
counter/order traces are submitted directly to Quint independently of semantic
prechecks. Actual stdout is retained beside each accepted ledger in
`native-validation.txt`.

The independent oracle builds per-condition ID sets from raw records and
intersects them, rather than calling the production evaluator to create expected
membership. It checks actual physical-presence probes, exact matching counts,
representative tie order/field proof, original scopes, typed SQL parameters,
bounded/no-data probes, waterfall rows and comparison, DOM UTC/ms/qualified
fields, local-action effects and closed-editor/held-response ownership. Model
pairs replay only the observed 10-action main and eight-action owner schedules;
they do not prove all interleavings, telemetry completeness or unique invocations.

No further campaigns are planned after these passes. Final documentation/link
checks, whole-milestone review and publication follow below.

## Whole-product review and roadmap revision (2026-10-25)

Six agents reviewed shipped baseline `9e07b88` across product, architecture,
usability, platform, verification and planning reconciliation. Reviews inspect
source, existing contracts, prior proposals and committed frames/acceptance;
they are not new browser sessions, customer studies, performance measurements
or source integration campaigns. No runtime tests were needed for this
documentation-only round, and no previous pass is presented as a new run.

The [new roadmap](docs/ROADMAP_2026_10_25.md) and
[execution proposal](docs/BACKLOG_2026_10_25.md) replace the prior persistence-first
suggestion. Generic-source startup and routine CI are parallel enablers; explicit
HTTP server-span observation grain is the first functional outcome, followed by
native interval-to-record context. Lean aggregate projection requires a measured
read-set audit. Trace-search definition reuse is a conditional later cut, and
exact observed-result custody remains a separate contract. New cuts are proposed,
not started or automatically authorized by this review request.

The plan preserves shipped discovery/native/trace/evidence work rather than
rescheduling old proposals. It makes high-cardinality, pipeline-source, training,
collaboration, warehouse and shared-deployment prerequisites explicit. Current
README/implementation links point to the new index; old Oct24 files are clearly
historical. Known Issues corrections align local draft recovery, counted-value
lookup, typed numeric measures and the 76-operation/70-Go API inventory with
existing implemented contracts. No application, API, CI or data behavior changes.

Independent planning review and a second architecture sanity review **APPROVE**
the integrated priority, scope, dependencies and factual corrections. Initial
local validation checks 564 relative links across 13 changed Markdown files,
all six review inputs and the new reconciliation anchor; whitespace checks pass.
The final publication checks include this validation entry separately.


## Automatic dashboard roadmap clarification (2026-10-25)

The user clarified the main product initiative: automatic USE/RED and other
role-specific dashboards for discovered OTel services and Kubernetes/database/
web/messaging resources. C2 is expanded from a single observation lens to
capability-driven dashboard families with staged C2a–C2d deliveries. HTTP is
the first vertical, followed explicitly by RPC, database/messaging, pool/runtime/
host resource prerequisites and Kubernetes entity discovery/views.

The [family plan](docs/AUTOMATIC_DASHBOARDS.md) specifies useful panels, partial-
data behavior, discovery-driven defaults, bounded evaluation and investigation
continuations without requiring manifests or dashboard authoring. It separates
existing service/native/catalog foundations from proposed full-family execution.
Source review confirms fractional gauges already work; fractional counters,
nonmonotonic sum handling and non-service resource identity/discovery are actual
remaining prerequisites for selected panels. No existing service identity
validation is globally relaxed by the proposal.

Product and architecture agents appended the user's clarification to their
reviews, preserving their original review conclusions. Architecture sanity
review approves the expanded contract; its requested explicit RPC substage is
included. README, service overview guide, roadmap, backlog and implementation
index now distinguish current automatic overviews from the proposed family
program. All 296 initially checked relative links across eight changed Markdown
files resolve and whitespace checks pass. This is documentation/planning only;
no application changes, browser sessions or runtime tests were performed.
