## C1 generic-source startup — reviewed source (2026-10-25)

Go `serve-http --profile generic` no longer requires demo bindings, inventory,
instrumentation scope or a default checkout operation package. The existing lab
profile remains the default. Explicit optional prepared flags are all-or-none;
invalid, missing or malformed explicitly configured packages fail startup. Pure
configuration metadata reports enabled optional families without probing telemetry.
Five unconfigured family GET routes return typed 503 refusals rather than a healthy
empty inventory. Historical investigation GET still reopens saved bytes, while
refresh without the installed package retains its existing 409 behavior.

Python `GOO_PROFILE=generic` starts without importing grammar or reading disabled
sample assets. UI/API assets and existing Go proxies remain available. Independent
UI and Go capability metadata describe configuration, not source connectivity or
health. The browser gates optional examples/prepared controls and distinguishes an
unconfigured declaration registry from transport failure. Generic source, native
and saved-work execution owners and custody formats are unchanged.

Focused startup tests include an empty-cwd child process, absent/partial/explicit-
empty/invalid configuration and preserved optional packages. Broad `cmd/goo` tests
pass (0.858s). Startup/proxy Python checks pass 13 tests (1.67s), including an
asset-only runtime without grammar, definitions or examples; socket-dependent
checks used authorized local loopback access. All 78 Node files pass (7.29s), and
inline bootstrap syntax checks pass. OpenAPI validates 78 operations (71 Go),
408 schemas, 288 responses, 93 admitted requests and 16 mux refusals. Counting
source controls verify capability metadata and unconfigured refusals add zero
source reads. Earlier frozen response fixtures remain unchanged.

Independent final source review approves the cut. Existing finite configuration
and boundary checks cover this startup change; no duplicate property quota or
new lifecycle model is introduced. The isolated real-source browser/capture and
ordinary non-Go OTel workflow remain separate live acceptance gates below.


The first isolated browser attempt exposed a real startup defect before fixture
ingestion: the existing Activity bootstrap still supplied the lab's `local`
environment and automatically read logs. That failed run is retained under
`artifacts/generic-startup/first-failure-diagnostics`, with its owned subprocesses
stopped and temporary runtime removed. It is not accepted as source-free startup.
The correction waits for UI configuration, gives generic/unknown profiles a blank
environment and explicit Start stream, and keeps lab automatic entry unchanged.
Signal/environment edits before the first applied selector remain local; inert
Resume controls without a selector are hidden. Focused entry/tail tests and all
78 Node files pass (7.84s). Independent review approves this correction before
another frozen live invocation.


The second isolated attempt confirms blank scope, explicit Start and no automatic
POST/tail request, then rejects a remaining example-catalog GET from the guided
workflow widget before ingestion. Its diagnostic and cleanup receipt are retained
separately. Guided workflows now await the same configuration: unconfigured or
unknown examples skip both example catalogs while user-owned measurement package
metadata remains available. Status distinguishes unconfigured from unavailable;
no healthy zero-workflow claim is made. Independent review and syntax checks
approve this additional gate before the next frozen invocation.

## C3 native interval to record context — reviewed source (2026-10-25)

The first handoff uses actual counter/histogram export intervals in the live
Measurements inspector. It stages ordinary Logs/Spans questions; Run is the
existing explicit source read. Gauge observations and aggregated histogram time
buckets have no new handoff. No nearby window is invented. Unknown/reset intervals
can supply time context without becoming healthy measurements or causal evidence.

Pure Go/JavaScript mappers validate the parent question and selected exact stream.
They copy only established environment, service, resource namespace (missing,
empty or value) and instance identity. Metric name, metric point dimensions,
instrumentation scope/version and schema URL do not become record filters.
Floor/ceil millisecond bounds form an enclosing query window; exact `timestamp_ns`
`gte fromNS` and `lte toNS-1` predicates preserve half-open membership. Invalid,
out-of-parent or over-wide enclosing windows refuse rather than using current time.
Existing API, SQL operators, storage/custody and capture formats remain unchanged.

The root application owner retains the accepted native DOM/selected interval and
opener while staging a record question. Explicit Back or browser Back restores it
without automatically previewing, polling or rerunning the native question. Hidden,
closed, detached, stale or inactive controls cannot act; modified/new-tab links keep
normal browser behavior. Trace expansion intentionally retains its existing fresh
reported-trace-ID read in the enclosing millisecond window, potentially broader
in time/resource scope than the exact interval. The origin question and visible
broader-context explanation remain available; no exemplar or contributing-span
relationship is inferred.

Source checks pass: exploration regressions (1.082s), 19 shared Go/JS fixtures and
21 additional JS boundary/identity checks, 24 independently generated Hegel exact-
ns grids plus finite refusals (0.037s), all 80 Node files (3.73s), and final focused
owner/measurement guard checks (0.28s). Independent final source review approves
frozen browser acceptance. Actual source membership, native return/focus,
cancellation and trace scope remain separate live gates below.

## C4 aggregate read-set audit (2026-10-25)

size/hash, consumed decoded response bytes, timings and allowlisted numeric
ClickHouse summary fields without emitting source rows or SQL parameters.

the final tool whitelists canonical uint64 strings, and both live cases were
rerun after that correction.

This is the prerequisite baseline, not a projection or performance improvement.
The existing generated OTLP fixture was reused without writes. Background lab
traffic remained active, so source read-row counts differ between cases; a fresh
controlled before/after comparison is required for implementation. No app/API/UI
behavior or capture bytes changed; browser/property/model campaigns were not
needed for this standalone audit. Counted values is the selected first adoption;
the service lens needs a separate richer summary projection.

is unknown. Exact-decimal guards refuse values that would round into supported
integral phases. Generic categorical capture comparisons explicitly return
unsupported before arithmetic. New optional fields preserve legacy serialization.

## 204a — reported container authority and readiness API prerequisite

Pinned Contrib v0.137.0 metadata and actual pod/container emitters were read from
upstream before implementation. Ready is an empty-unit integer gauge recorded
from ContainerStatus.Ready, with reported container.id, pod UID and container name.
The emitter strips runtime prefixes and omits empty-ID statuses; goo consumes
reported IDs verbatim, never repeats that normalization or synthesizes an ID.
A reported Ready does not establish that a probe exists or that a pod is healthy.
Links and exact scope are in [the contract](docs/CONTAINER_READINESS.md) and
[API storyboard](journeys/204a-container-readiness-storyboard.md).

Added state-only exact container authority, pinned readiness profile and shared
private categorical quality/decode kernel. Supported 0/1 labels remain separate
from observed/stale/unknown quality; unsupported, conflicting, missing and empty
source remain distinct. Source precision checks now use supported profile/code
membership, including zero: tiny nonzero decimals cannot round into Not ready.
Existing scalar units, pod authority/profile, full raw resource/schema/attribute
isolation, row budgets and comparison refusal remain unchanged. YAML source
canonicalization extends only to the explicit new resource kind.

Affected suites pass: metrics 0.329s, measurements 0.499s, exploration 1.470s,
cmd/goo 1.308s. Focused readiness/phase tests passed before these; new actual-handler
execute/capture/reopen test passes in 0.021s. It uses the real handlers/compiler/
evaluator and temporary filesystem custody with a controlled dependency
transport: exactly two source reads (execute and fresh capture), then a fresh
offline handler reopens with zero source reads and full stored bytes unchanged.
Code 0 remains observed Not ready, comparison refuses state arithmetic, and an
unsupported profile fails before source access. No actual collector/browser or
real ClickHouse outage is claimed by this API-level test.

Hegel's new 60-case container identity/code/freshness property plus prior phase
quality/replay properties pass together in 0.393s. Replacement-ID, decimal-underflow
and invented-label controls are rejected. The prior accepted pod-phase actual
raw/custody oracle and its twelve controls replay source-free in 0.505s after the
kernel refactor; the existing browser evidence is not recaptured or changed.
OpenAPI remains 87 operations / 495 schemas, with additive container kind/readiness
labels/profile descriptions. Full validation passes 80 Go operations, 395 fixture
responses, 144 admitted requests, 25 mux refusals and existing negative/SSE controls;
four new actual-handler responses conform and capture/reopen equality passes.
Existing frozen fixtures are unchanged.

Independent design and implementation reviews approve with no blockers. The
review specifically required exact decimal validation of supported zero and

Initial sandbox curl could not reach the session proxy; supported escalation
